In April, Trump stated that Iranians want direct negotiations.[\[189\]](#cite%5Fnote-189) [Iran–United States negotiations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025–2026%5FIran–United%5FStates%5Fnegotiations "2025–2026 Iran–United States negotiations") began on April 12, 2025.[\[190\]](#cite%5Fnote-190)

In April 2025, U.S. congressmen [Joe Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joe%5FWilson "Joe Wilson") and [Jimmy Panetta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy%5FPanetta "Jimmy Panetta") introduced a 'Free Iraq from Iran' bill. The legislation mandates the development of a comprehensive U.S. strategy to irreversibly dismantle Iran-backed militias, including the [Popular Mobilization Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular%5FMobilization%5FForces "Popular Mobilization Forces") (PMF), and calls for the suspension of U.S. assistance to Iraq until these militias are fully removed. The bill also imposes sanctions on Iraqi political and military figures aligned with Iran, and provides support for Iraqi citizens and independent media to expose abuses committed by these militias. Its primary objective is to restore Iraq's sovereignty and reduce Iranian dominance without resorting to direct military intervention.[\[191\]](#cite%5Fnote-191)

In his speech during [his May 2025 trip to Middle East](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May%5F2025%5Fvisit%5Fby%5FDonald%5FTrump%5Fto%5Fthe%5FMiddle%5FEast "May 2025 visit by Donald Trump to the Middle East"), Trump called out the Iranians as the most destructive force and denounced the Iran leaders for having managed to be turning green fertile farmland and rolling blackouts calling on it to make a choice between war and violence and making a deal.[\[192\]](#cite%5Fnote-192)[\[193\]](#cite%5Fnote-193)On June 7, 2025, the [U.S. Treasury Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.%5FTreasury%5FDepartment "U.S. Treasury Department") imposed sanctions on 10 individuals and 27 entities, including Iranian nationals and firms based in the [UAE](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UAE "UAE") and [Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong%5FKong "Hong Kong"). These targets include the Zarringhalam brothers, accused of [laundering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Money%5Flaundering "Money laundering") billions via shell companies tied to the IRGC and the [Central Bank of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central%5FBank%5Fof%5FIran "Central Bank of Iran"). The funds reportedly supported Iran's nuclear and missile programs, oil sales, and militant proxies.[\[194\]](#cite%5Fnote-194)[\[195\]](#cite%5Fnote-195)

Relations further worsened after Iranian authorities threatened to attack U.S. and allied bases in the Middle East if the former were to involve itself in the [Iran–Israel war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day%5FWar "Twelve-Day War"),[\[196\]](#cite%5Fnote-196) after which the U.S. intervened in the Israeli attacks and bombed key Iranian nuclear sites.[\[197\]](#cite%5Fnote-197)

On June 21, 2025, the U.S., under the orders of Trump, [bombed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025%5FUnited%5FStates%5Fstrikes%5Fon%5FIranian%5Fnuclear%5Fsites "2025 United States strikes on Iranian nuclear sites") three Iranian nuclear enrichment sites ([Fordow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fordow%5FFuel%5FEnrichment%5FPlant "Fordow Fuel Enrichment Plant"), [Natanz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natanz%5FNuclear%5FFacility "Natanz Nuclear Facility"), and [Isfahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isfahan "Isfahan")), briefly joining the [Iran–Israel war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-Day%5FWar "Twelve-Day War").[\[198\]](#cite%5Fnote-198) Following the attacks, Iran pulled out and suspended nuclear talks indefinitely.[\[199\]](#cite%5Fnote-199)

[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:US%5Fbases%5Fin%5Fthe%5FMiddle%5FEast.svg)

US bases in the Middle East

During the [2025–2026 Iranian protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025–2026%5FIranian%5Fprotests "2025–2026 Iranian protests"), Trump repeatedly stated to the Iranian authorities that the U.S. would "intervene" if the Iranian government did not halt its [crackdown on protesters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FIran%5Fmassacres "2026 Iran massacres").[\[200\]](#cite%5Fnote-200)

In January 2026, during the widespread and increasingly violent crackdown on nationwide protests in Iran, Trump stated that the U.S. was seriously considering a range of responses, including potential military options, if Iranian government actions crossed established "red lines". Trump said that the U.S. military was evaluating "very strong options" and that senior advisers were scheduled to discuss possible actions, while also noting that Iranian leaders had reached out for negotiations. Iranian officials stated that Iranian forces would retaliate against U.S. forces and allied interests in the region if U.S. forces attacked.[\[201\]](#cite%5Fnote-201)On January 16, 2026, Trump announced that the Iranian leadership had reportedly canceled over 800 planned [executions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital%5Fpunishment%5Fin%5FIran "Capital punishment in Iran").[\[202\]](#cite%5Fnote-202) On January 27, 2026, following the deployment of the [USS _Abraham Lincoln_](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS%5FAbraham%5FLincoln%5F%28CVN-72%29 "USS Abraham Lincoln (CVN-72)") to the region the day before, a multi-day U.S. aerial military drill in the region was announced.[\[203\]](#cite%5Fnote-203)On the same day Trump said: "There is another beautiful war fleet sailing toward Iran right now."[\[204\]](#cite%5Fnote-204)[\[205\]](#cite%5Fnote-:0-205)[\[206\]](#cite%5Fnote-:1-206) In addition, [The Pentagon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The%5FPentagon "The Pentagon") ordered U.S. Air Force F-15E Strike Eagles that were deployed at [Royal Air Force Lakenheath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RAF%5FLakenheath "RAF Lakenheath") in Britain to move forward to American air bases in [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan "Jordan").[\[205\]](#cite%5Fnote-:0-205) The [January 2026 U.S. military buildup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FUnited%5FStates%5Fmilitary%5Fbuildup%5Fin%5Fthe%5FMiddle%5FEast "2026 United States military buildup in the Middle East") has been the largest concentration of air and naval assets in the region since the [2003 invasion of Iraq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2003%5Finvasion%5Fof%5FIraq "2003 invasion of Iraq").[\[206\]](#cite%5Fnote-:1-206)

In February 2026, following the heightened tensions in January, Iran and the United States held a new round of diplomatic talks in [Muscat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muscat "Muscat"), Oman, with Oman acting as a mediator. The discussions primarily focused on Iran's nuclear program and the potential easing of economic sanctions. The Iranian delegation was led by Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi, while the U.S. delegation included senior officials from the State Department.[\[207\]](#cite%5Fnote-207)[\[208\]](#cite%5Fnote-208)[\[209\]](#cite%5Fnote-209)

During the talks, the United States reportedly sought to address Iran's missile program and regional activities, whereas Iran emphasized that negotiations should remain limited to nuclear issues and sanctions relief. Both sides expressed commitment to continuing diplomatic engagement, though no immediate agreement was announced.[\[210\]](#cite%5Fnote-210) Reports showed that Iran may have unbelievable flexibility towards further discussions in averting war.[\[211\]](#cite%5Fnote-211)

The renewed dialogue was widely interpreted as an effort by both sides to prevent further escalation following the January tensions, amid concerns that miscalculation could lead to a broader regional confrontation.[\[212\]](#cite%5Fnote-212)[\[213\]](#cite%5Fnote-213)

#### 2026 Iran war

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=31%20%22Edit%20section:%202026%20Iran%20war%22)\]

Main article: [2026 Iran war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FIran%5Fwar "2026 Iran war")

[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Shajareh%5FTayyebeh%5Fschool%5Fin%5FMinab%5Fphotos%5Ffrom%5FMehr%5F%283%29.jpg)

Rescuers and residents searching through the rubble of the [Minab girls' elementary school](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FMinab%5Fschool%5Fattack "2026 Minab school attack"), destroyed by the United States

On February 28, 2026, the United States and Israel launched a [large-scale joint military operation against Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FIran%5Fwar "2026 Iran war"), codenamed _Operation Epic Fury_ by the U.S. and _Operation Roaring Lion_ by Israel.[\[214\]](#cite%5Fnote-war-of-choice-214) Trump announced the operation in a video statement, stating the objectives were to destroy Iran's ballistic missile program, prevent Iran from obtaining nuclear weapons, and ultimately bring about [regime change](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5Finvolvement%5Fin%5Fregime%5Fchange "United States involvement in regime change") in Iran.[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc-attack-215) Israeli prime minister [Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin%5FNetanyahu "Benjamin Netanyahu") said the strikes aimed to remove "the existential threat posed by the terrorist regime in Iran."[\[216\]](#cite%5Fnote-216) The [Israel Defense Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%5FDefense%5FForces "Israel Defense Forces") stated that approximately 200 fighter jets struck around 500 targets across Iran, including air defense systems, missile launchers, and command-and-control facilities.[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc-attack-215) Strikes were reported in at least 14 Iranian cities, including [Tehran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehran "Tehran"), [Isfahan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isfahan "Isfahan"), [Qom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qom "Qom"), [Karaj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karaj "Karaj"), and [Kermanshah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kermanshah "Kermanshah").[\[217\]](#cite%5Fnote-217) An [Iranian Red Crescent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5FRed%5FCrescent "Iranian Red Crescent") spokesperson reported that 24 of [Iran's 31 provinces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces%5Fof%5FIran "Provinces of Iran") were affected, with at least 201 people killed, [including Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination%5Fof%5FAli%5FKhamenei "Assassination of Ali Khamenei"), and 747 injured as of that evening.[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc-attack-215)Iran retaliated by launching ballistic missiles and drones at Israel and at U.S. military bases in five Gulf states—[Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar "Qatar"), the [United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FArab%5FEmirates "United Arab Emirates"), [Bahrain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahrain "Bahrain"), [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait "Kuwait"), and [Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan "Jordan")—as well as strikes in [Saudi Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saudi%5FArabia "Saudi Arabia").[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc-attack-215) The [Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") also announced the closure of the [Strait of Hormuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strait%5Fof%5FHormuz "Strait of Hormuz") to shipping.[\[215\]](#cite%5Fnote-bbc-attack-215) [United Nations Secretary-General](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FNations%5FSecretary-General "United Nations Secretary-General") [António Guterres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/António%5FGuterres "António Guterres") condemned both the U.S.–Israeli strikes and Iran's retaliation, stating they "undermine international peace and security," and called for an immediate cessation of hostilities.[\[218\]](#cite%5Fnote-218)

On April 3, 2026, it was reported in [Axios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axios%5F%28website%29 "Axios (website)") that the U.S. expelled Iran's deputy ambassador to the UN, in December 2025\. The issue remained silenced as it concerned national security. The report said that deputy ambassador Saadat Aghajani was asked to leave the U.S. under "section 13 procedures", rather than declaring him [persona non grata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persona%5Fnon%5Fgrata "Persona non grata").[\[219\]](#cite%5Fnote-219) On April 7, [Katie Miller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katie%5FMiller "Katie Miller") said that the U.S. was planning to revoke up to 4,000 visas of people with connections to the Iranian government.[\[220\]](#cite%5Fnote-220)

Following Donald Trump's March 2026 assertion that it was not "appropriate" for Iran to participate in the [2026 World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2026%5FWorld%5FCup "2026 World Cup") due to safety concerns amid military conflict, the Iran national team issued a statement arguing that no one could exclude them and that the United States should instead be removed as host for failing to ensure the safety of participating teams.[\[221\]](#cite%5Fnote-221)

In April 2026, U.S. special envoy [Paolo Zampolli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paolo%5FZampolli "Paolo Zampolli") proposed that Italy replace Iran at the 2026 FIFA World Cup, prompting swift rejection from Italian officials who called the idea "not appropriate" and "shameful." Italian Sports Minister [Andrea Abodi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrea%5FAbodi "Andrea Abodi") stated that qualification "must be earned on the pitch," while FIFA president [Gianni Infantino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gianni%5FInfantino "Gianni Infantino") reiterated that Iran would participate as scheduled.[\[222\]](#cite%5Fnote-222)[\[223\]](#cite%5Fnote-223)

On April 8, 2026, the United States and Iran agreed to a provisional two-week ceasefire ahead of planned negotiations in Pakistan. As part of the agreement, Tehran stated it would reopen the Strait of Hormuz to maritime traffic. Following the announcement of the conditional ceasefire, global [oil prices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil%5Fprice "Oil price") experienced a sharp decline.[\[224\]](#cite%5Fnote-224)

In April 2026, the Iranian-flagged container ship MV Touska was intercepted en route from Malaysia to Iran. According to Iranian sources and state media, the vessel was operating near the Gulf of Oman when it was targeted by United States naval forces as part of what they described as enforcement of a maritime blockade.[\[225\]](#cite%5Fnote-225) Iranian reports stated that the ship was struck by the USS _Spruance_, which allegedly disabled its propulsion by firing on the engine room, after which U.S. Marines boarded and took control of the vessel. Iranian officials and media characterized the incident as an attack on a civilian commercial vessel and a violation of an existing ceasefire agreement.[\[226\]](#cite%5Fnote-226)[\[227\]](#cite%5Fnote-227)

According to statements from U.S. officials, the operation was conducted after warnings were issued to the vessel, which was alleged to be violating sanctions and attempting to breach a naval blockade. The U.S. administration stated that the ship was declared subject to sanctions and that its seizure was part of enforcement actions in the region.[\[228\]](#cite%5Fnote-228) Iranian authorities further claimed that the incident triggered retaliatory actions in the region and cited it as evidence of a breakdown of the ceasefire arrangements.[\[229\]](#cite%5Fnote-229)[\[230\]](#cite%5Fnote-230)

According to data from Brown University's Iran War Energy Cost Tracker, as reported by Anadolu Agency on May 12, 2026, the war had cost American consumers more than $37.3 billion in additional energy prices since February 28, 2026.[\[231\]](#cite%5Fnote-231)[\[232\]](#cite%5Fnote-232)

According to a classified United States intelligence assessment from early May 2026, Iran has regained operational access to most of its missile sites. This includes 30 out of the 33 missile sites located along the Strait of Hormuz.[\[233\]](#cite%5Fnote-233)

After more than three months of war, the United States and Iran announced on June 14, 2026, that they had agreed to halt hostilities. Mediated by Pakistan and [Qatar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qatar "Qatar"), the [memorandum of understanding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memorandum%5Fof%5Funderstanding "Memorandum of understanding") was presented not as a final peace treaty but as a reported 60-day extension of the existing ceasefire, intended to lay the groundwork for a final settlement.[\[17\]](#cite%5Fnote-axios-mou-17)[\[18\]](#cite%5Fnote-wapo-framework-18) Under its terms, the United States lifted its naval blockade and the [Strait of Hormuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strait%5Fof%5FHormuz "Strait of Hormuz"), closed for much of the war, was to be cleared of mines and reopened to shipping, while the 60-day window was set aside to negotiate limits on Iran's nuclear program, the disposal of its highly enriched uranium, sanctions relief, and the release of frozen Iranian assets.[\[17\]](#cite%5Fnote-axios-mou-17)[\[234\]](#cite%5Fnote-nbc-deal-234) President Donald Trump and Pakistani Prime Minister [Shehbaz Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shehbaz%5FSharif "Shehbaz Sharif") characterized the agreement as a peace deal, and a formal signing ceremony was expected in Switzerland; Iranian officials were initially more cautious about confirming it, and some analysts in the United States and Israel doubted that a final agreement would follow.[\[235\]](#cite%5Fnote-aj-ceasefire-235)[\[17\]](#cite%5Fnote-axios-mou-17)

In June 2026, [Fox News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox%5FNews "Fox News") Digital reported that the Trump administration was asking for $672 million as part of a larger $80 billion funding package linked to the Iran conflict, as discussions continued over a memorandum of understanding on Iran’s nuclear program. The funding would go toward removing and managing Iranian nuclear materials, including different forms of uranium, as well as inspection and verification work. A [White House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White%5FHouse "White House") official said the money would support Department of Energy operations aimed at limiting Iran’s nuclear capabilities. This would include handling sensitive materials and infrastructure, supporting [International Atomic Energy Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International%5FAtomic%5FEnergy%5FAgency "International Atomic Energy Agency") inspections, improving nuclear smuggling detection, and expanding emergency response teams in the Middle East.[\[236\]](#cite%5Fnote-236)

In early July 2026, the June ceasefire memorandum appeared to begin breaking down. On July 9, 2026, the [Associated Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Associated%5FPress "Associated Press") reported that renewed U.S. strikes in Iran and Iranian missile and drone attacks across the Gulf were threatening the ceasefire arrangement. The AP described the truce as increasingly unstable after both sides resumed attacks in the region, raising doubts about whether the June agreement would hold.[\[237\]](#cite%5Fnote-237)[\[238\]](#cite%5Fnote-238)

In July 2026, Iran's UN ambassador [Amir Saeid Iravani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir-Saeid%5FIravani "Amir-Saeid Iravani") wrote to the UN Secretary-General and Security Council, accusing the U.S. of war crimes over military strikes on Iranian soil. The letter described attacks between July 8 and 16 that hit civilian infrastructure such as ports, airports, transport networks, communications hubs, radar and coastal defense systems, and grain silos. Iravani reported more than 35 dead and over 260 wounded, including civilians and first responders. He also cited President Trump's public threats against Iranian bridges and power plants, along with remarks about bombing Iran's nuclear sites, as proof of what he called "blatant disregard for the Charter of the United Nations and international law."[\[239\]](#cite%5Fnote-239)

### 2026 US MoU Iranian nuclear agreement

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=32%20%22Edit%20section:%202026%20US%20MoU%20Iranian%20nuclear%20agreement%22)\]

On July 3rd President Trump declared Iran has agreed to everything and almostly all and every one of US demands. [\[240\]](#cite%5Fnote-240)

## Economic relations

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=33%20%22Edit%20section:%20Economic%20relations%22)\]

See also: [United States sanctions against Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5Fsanctions%5Fagainst%5FIran "United States sanctions against Iran"), [Economy of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FUnited%5FStates "Economy of the United States"), [Economy of Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy%5Fof%5FIran "Economy of Iran"), [Agriculture in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agriculture%5Fin%5FIran "Agriculture in Iran"), [Healthcare in Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare%5Fin%5FIran "Healthcare in Iran"), and [Iran–United States copyright relations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran–United%5FStates%5Fcopyright%5Frelations "Iran–United States copyright relations")

Trade between Iran and the United States reached $623 million in 2008\. According to the [United States Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5FCensus%5FBureau "United States Census Bureau"), American exports to Iran reached $93 million in 2007 and $537 million in 2008\. American imports from Iran decreased from $148 million in 2007 to $86 million in 2008.[\[241\]](#cite%5Fnote-iran-daily.com-241) This data does not include trade conducted through third countries to circumvent the [trade embargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5Fsanctions%5Fagainst%5FIran "United States sanctions against Iran"). It has been reported that the [United States Treasury Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FStates%5FTreasury%5FDepartment "United States Treasury Department") has granted nearly 10,000 special licenses to American companies over the past decade to conduct business with Iran.[\[242\]](#cite%5Fnote-242)

US exports to Iran include\[_[when?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual%5Fof%5FStyle/Dates%5Fand%5Fnumbers#Chronological%5Fitems "Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers")_\] cigarettes (US$73 million); corn (US$68 million); chemical wood pulp, soda or sulfate (US$64 million); soybeans (US$43 million); medical equipment (US$27 million); vitamins (US$18 million); and vegetable seeds (US$12 million).[\[241\]](#cite%5Fnote-iran-daily.com-241)

In May 2013, U.S. president [Barack Obama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barack%5FObama "Barack Obama") lifted a trade [embargo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Embargo "Embargo") of communications equipment and software to non-government Iranians.[\[243\]](#cite%5Fnote-243) In June 2013, the Obama administration expanded its sanctions against Iran, targeting its auto industry and, for the first time, its currency.[\[244\]](#cite%5Fnote-244)

[](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Fourth%5Fmatch%5Fbetween%5FIran%5Fand%5FThe%5FUnited%5FStates%5Fnational%5Fvolleyball%5Fteams%5Fin%5F2015%5FFIVB%5FVolleyball%5FWorld%5FLeague%5F%287%29.jpg)

Fourth match between Iran and the U.S. during the [2015 FIVB Volleyball World League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015%5FFIVB%5FVolleyball%5FWorld%5FLeague "2015 FIVB Volleyball World League")

According to a 2014 study by the [National Iranian American Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National%5FIranian%5FAmerican%5FCouncil "National Iranian American Council"), sanctions cost the U.S. over $175 billion in lost trade and 279,000 lost job opportunities.[\[245\]](#cite%5Fnote-245)

According to _Business Monitor International_:

> The tentative rapprochement between Iran and the US, which began in the second half of 2013, has the potential to become a world-changing development, and unleash tremendous geopolitical and economic opportunities, if it is sustained. Tehran and Washington have been bitter enemies since 1979, when the Iranian Revolution overthrew the pro-American Shah and replaced him with a virulently anti-American Islamist regime. Since then, Iran has been at the vanguard of countries actively challenging the US-led world order. This has led to instability in the Middle East, and Iran's relative isolation in international affairs. Yet, if Iran and the US were to achieve a diplomatic breakthrough, geopolitical tensions in the Middle East could decline sharply, and Iran could come to be perceived as a promising emerging market in its own right.[\[246\]](#cite%5Fnote-246)

On April 22, 2019, under the Trump administration, the U.S. demanded that buyers of Iranian oil stop purchases or face economic penalties, announcing that the six-month sanction exemptions for China, India, Japan, South Korea and Turkey instated a year prior would not be renewed and would end by May 1\. The move was seen as an attempt to completely stifle [Iran's oil exports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oil%5Freserves%5Fin%5FIran "Oil reserves in Iran"). Iran insisted the sanctions were illegal and that it had attached "no value or credibility" to the waivers. U.S. Secretary of State [Mike Pompeo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mike%5FPompeo "Mike Pompeo") said President Trump's decision not to renew the waivers showed his administration was "dramatically accelerating our pressure campaign in a calibrated way that meets our national security objectives while maintaining well supplied global oil markets".[\[247\]](#cite%5Fnote-247) On April 30, Iran stated it would continue to export oil despite U.S. pressure.[\[248\]](#cite%5Fnote-248)

On May 8, 2019, exactly one year after the Trump administration withdrew the United States from the [Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint%5FComprehensive%5FPlan%5Fof%5FAction "Joint Comprehensive Plan of Action"), the U.S. imposed a new layer of duplicate sanctions on Iran, targeting its metal exports, a sector that generates 10 percent of its export revenue. The move came amid escalating tension in the region and just hours after Iran threatened to start enriching more uranium if it did not get relief from U.S. measures that are crippling its economy. The Trump administration has said the sanctions will only be lifted if Iran fundamentally changes its behavior and character.[\[249\]](#cite%5Fnote-249)

On June 24, 2019, following continued escalations in the [Strait of Hormuz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strait%5Fof%5FHormuz "Strait of Hormuz"), President Donald Trump announced new targeted sanctions against Iranian and [IRGC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IRGC "IRGC") leadership, including [Supreme Leader](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme%5FLeader%5Fof%5FIran "Supreme Leader of Iran") [Ali Khamenei](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ali%5FKhamenei "Ali Khamenei") and his [office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office%5Fof%5Fthe%5FSupreme%5FLeader%5Fof%5FIran "Office of the Supreme Leader of Iran"). IRGC targets included [Naval](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Navy%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Navy of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") commander [Alireza Tangsiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alireza%5FTangsiri "Alireza Tangsiri"), [Aerospace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerospace%5FForce%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Aerospace Force of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") commander [Amir Ali Hajizadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amir%5FAli%5FHajizadeh "Amir Ali Hajizadeh"), and [Ground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground%5FForces%5Fof%5Fthe%5FIslamic%5FRevolutionary%5FGuard%5FCorps "Ground Forces of the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps") commander [Mohammad Pakpour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohammad%5FPakpour "Mohammad Pakpour"), and others.[\[250\]](#cite%5Fnote-250)[\[251\]](#cite%5Fnote-251) [U.S. Treasury Secretary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S.%5FTreasury%5FSecretary "U.S. Treasury Secretary") [Steven Mnuchin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven%5FMnuchin "Steven Mnuchin") said the sanctions will block "billions" in assets.[\[252\]](#cite%5Fnote-252)

The U.S. Treasury Department [Financial Crimes Enforcement Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial%5FCrimes%5FEnforcement%5FNetwork "Financial Crimes Enforcement Network") imposed a measure that further prohibits the U.S. banking system from use by an Iranian bank, thereby requiring U.S. banks to step up due diligence on the accounts in their custody.[\[253\]](#cite%5Fnote-fincen%5F5th%5Fmeasure%5Firan-253)

The U.S. Department of Treasury's [Office of Foreign Assets Control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office%5Fof%5FForeign%5FAssets%5FControl "Office of Foreign Assets Control") (OFAC) blacklisted four Iranian metal sector organizations along with their foreign subsidiaries, on July 23, 2020\. One German subsidiary and three in the [United Arab Emirates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United%5FArab%5FEmirates "United Arab Emirates")—owned and controlled by Iran's biggest steel manufacturer, [Mobarakeh Steel Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobarakeh%5FSteel%5FCompany "Mobarakeh Steel Company")—were also blacklisted by Washington for yielding millions of dollars for Iran's aluminum, steel, iron, and copper sectors. The sanctions froze all U.S. assets controlled by the companies in question and further prohibited Americans from associating with them.[\[254\]](#cite%5Fnote-254)[\[255\]](#cite%5Fnote-255)

On October 8, 2020, the U.S. Treasury Department placed sanctions on 18 Iranian banks. Any American connection to these banks is to be blocked and reported to the Office of Foreign Assets Control, and, 45 days after the sanctions take effect, anyone transacting with these banks may "be subject to an enforcement action." Treasury Secretary [Steven Mnuchin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steven%5FMnuchin "Steven Mnuchin") said the goal was to pressure Iran to end nuclear activities and terrorist funding.[\[256\]](#cite%5Fnote-256)

On October 30, 2020, it was revealed that the U.S. had "seized Iranian missiles shipped to [Yemen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yemen "Yemen")", and it had "sold 1.1 million barrels of previously seized Iranian oil that was bound for [Venezuela](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venezuela "Venezuela")" in two shipments: the [Liberia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberia "Liberia")\-flagged _Euroforce_ and [Singapore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singapore "Singapore")\-flagged _[Maersk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maersk "Maersk") Progress_, and sanctioned 11 new Iranian entities.[\[257\]](#cite%5Fnote-snlgm-257)

In February 2026, Donald Trump issued an executive order imposing tariffs of up to 25 percent on nations engaged in trade with Iran. The policy focuses on Iran's commercial partners rather than the country itself and aims to reduce international transactions with Iran, even as diplomatic talks continued.[\[258\]](#cite%5Fnote-258)[\[259\]](#cite%5Fnote-259)

## Sources of the socio-political tensions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=34%20%22Edit%20section:%20Sources%20of%20the%20socio-political%20tensions%22)\]

The antagonism has been explained in part by an incompatibility of social values, the resistance to American hegemony, and its [US support for Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel–United%5FStates%5Frelations "Israel–United States relations").[\[260\]](#cite%5Fnote-260)[\[261\]](#cite%5Fnote-261) In the West, however, various explanations have been considered,[\[1\]](#cite%5Fnote-Ansari%5F2006-1) including the [Iranian government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5Fgovernment "Iranian government")'s need for an external [bogeyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogeyman "Bogeyman") to furnish a pretext for domestic repression against [pro-democratic forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian%5Freform%5Fmovement "Iranian reform movement") and to bind the government to its loyal constituency.[\[262\]](#cite%5Fnote-262) The United States attributes the worsening of relations to the 1979–81 [Iran hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%5Fhostage%5Fcrisis "Iran hostage crisis"),[\[1\]](#cite%5Fnote-Ansari%5F2006-1) Iran's repeated [human rights abuses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human%5Frights%5Fin%5FIran "Human rights in Iran") since the Islamic Revolution, different restrictions on using spy methods on democratic revolutions by the U.S., its anti-Western ideology, its [nuclear program](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear%5Fprogram%5Fof%5FIran "Nuclear program of Iran"), and political, financial, and military support to [armed groups committed primarily to countering the influence of U.S. and Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Axis%5Fof%5FResistance "Axis of Resistance") in the [West Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West%5FAsia "West Asia").[\[263\]](#cite%5Fnote-Hubbard-2024-263)[\[264\]](#cite%5Fnote-AlKassab-2023-264)[\[265\]](#cite%5Fnote-265)[\[266\]](#cite%5Fnote-266)

## Public opinion

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=35%20%22Edit%20section:%20Public%20opinion%22)\]

### Iranians

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=36%20%22Edit%20section:%20Iranians%22)\]

Research has shown that most Iranians hold a positive attitude about the American people, though not the U.S. government.[\[267\]](#cite%5Fnote-267)[\[268\]](#cite%5Fnote-268)

According to a 2019 survey by IranPoll, 13% of Iranians have a favorable view of the United States, with 86% expressing an unfavourable view, the most unfavorable perception of the United States in the world.[\[269\]](#cite%5Fnote-269)

According to the [University of Maryland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University%5Fof%5FMaryland,%5FCollege%5FPark "University of Maryland, College Park")'s School of Public Policy report _Iranian Public Opinion in the Early Days of the Pezeshkian Administration_ released in May 2025, public opinion in Iran toward the United States has shifted significantly over time. According to survey data, the perception of America as a dangerous country increased from 46% in 2005 to 66% in 2019, before slightly declining to 64% in March 2024\. Conversely, views of the United States as a model country for its values and freedoms declined sharply, from 37% in 2005 to 12% in 2019 and further to 9% in 2024\. Meanwhile, the proportion of respondents who considered America to be no better or worse than other countries rose steadily, from 14% in 2005 to 20% in 2019 and 25% in March 2024, indicating a gradual normalization alongside persistent negative perceptions.[\[270\]](#cite%5Fnote-CISSMIran2025Report-270)

### Americans

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Iran%E2%80%93United%5FStates%5Frelations&action=edit&section=37%20%22Edit%20section:%20Americans%22)\]

In [NORC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NORC%5Fat%5Fthe%5FUniversity%5Fof%5FChicago "NORC at the University of Chicago") polling conducted in 1952 ahead of the coup d'état the following year, 35% of Americans said that it would matter a "great deal" if communists took control of Iran. Public concern about Iran was generally limited before the 1979 [Iran hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%5Fhostage%5Fcrisis "Iran hostage crisis").[\[271\]](#cite%5Fnote-MaristIran-271) After the [September 11 attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/September%5F11%5Fattacks "September 11 attacks") made much of the public fearful of future acts of terrorism,[\[272\]](#cite%5Fnote-272)[\[273\]](#cite%5Fnote-273)[\[274\]](#cite%5Fnote-274) concerns about Iran intensified. In 2004, 58% of Americans said that they felt that Iran posed a long-term threat to the United States. Many Americans at the time viewed Iran unfavorably and thought it posed a threat to the United States and other countries.[\[271\]](#cite%5Fnote-MaristIran-271)

According to a 2018 [Pew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew%5FResearch%5FCenter "Pew Research Center") poll, 39% of Americans say that limiting the power and influence of Iran should be a top foreign policy priority.[\[275\]](#cite%5Fnote-275)

According to a 2013 [BBC World Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC%5FWorld%5FService "BBC World Service") poll, 5% of [Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Americans "Americans") view Iranian influence positively, with 87% expressing a negative view, the most unfavorable perception of Iran in the world.[\[276\]](#cite%5Fnote-276)

According to a 2026 Gallup poll, only 13% of Americans have favorable opinions of Iran, while 79% have an unfavorable opinion. This was the second most negative ranking Americans gave out of the 21 foreign countries surveyed.[\[277\]](#cite%5Fnote-277)

They all knew about Kermit Roosevelt's book _Countercoup_, and most of them had read extracts from it.
